 Every *blocks* value of *novelty* fell to *nothing* under §29.2.1 — six of the thirteen. **E(Q) = 0
 in all four domain fibres and 1 unfibred**, both printed per §32.1.1 flag 2. Register 256.
 E.1.3 What this index asserts, and what it recomputes
 F.3.3 establishes that a count computed from the book cannot be printed as prose, because writing the
 count changes what it counts. **The same holds here, and this appendix was making the same mistake.**

 E(Q) has been stated as *nought fibred and one unfibred*. Recomputed on the table as it now stands —
 item O closed, item K closed, item M restated at register 371 — **the unfibred figure is 4.** It moved
 because the table moved, and the table moves whenever an item closes or a cost is re-graded, which is
 the ordinary business of this appendix.

    **A count over an index of open questions is the least stable thing about it.** Every closure
    changes it, and closures are the point.

 **So this appendix asserts what does not move and computes what does.** What does not move: **E(Q) = 0
 under the fibration by domain**, a statement about the table's shape rather than its contents, which
 has held at seven, eight, twelve, thirteen and eleven items; and the standing result that **every item
 has depends = no**, which has held at every size and is the only column whose value has never changed.
 What does move, and is therefore recomputed rather than written, is the unfibred count and the item
 total.

 §12.11.3.1's rule applied here as F.3.3 applies it there — **assert the law, compute the extent.**
 Register 372.

 E.1.4 This is an index of tractable ignorance, and it cannot say otherwise
 Every item here is closable. The obstacle coordinate takes three values — **nonexistent** (the datum
 can be made to exist), **buildable** (it can be constructed), **retrievable** (it can be fetched) —
 and all three name a route. Eleven items: one nonexistent, two buildable, eight retrievable.

    **No value of this coordinate means *there is no route*.** An unclosable question is not
    missing from this appendix. It is **inexpressible** in the coordinates the appendix uses.

 **That is not an oversight, and §32.1.4 says why it cannot be repaired from inside.** ℛ(X) ⊆ ∏ᵢ Aᵢ(X):
 the operator is bounded by the index's own value sets, so it can name a cell the index lacks and never
 a *value*. Nothing in this appendix could ever produce the fourth obstacle, however long it ran.

 **Offer it from outside and the shape of what is missing appears.** Adding *no route* as a fourth
 obstacle value grows the ambient box from 18 cells to 24 and takes E from 4 to **10** — six further
 combinations the structure admits and this appendix does not contain, every one of them an unclosable
 question at some cost and some consequence.

 **So the book has two kinds of ignorance and indexes only one.** The tractable kind is here, graded and
 costed. The other kind is elsewhere and unindexed: §29.2.2's reading that the branches leave the
 object, and the destination coordinate that the temporal mechanisms would need. **Those are not open
 items. They are the shape of the question this index cannot pose**, and each had to be named in prose
 because no coordinate here would hold it.

 That is register 332 arriving where it is least comfortable — an index names cells and never
 coordinates, so **an index of one's own ignorance is an index of the ignorance one already has words
 for.** Register 382.

 E.2 Closed
 Do the perturbation bounds tighten at higher order? Yes, 1,585-fold — median 25.96 cm⁻¹ at
 order 1, 0.0164 at order 6. §23.10

 **Is there an order maximising the bound, given σ?** **No interior optimum.** Information per cell rises monotonically; the maximum is the admissibility cap *k*\*, computable per cell. §23.11

 **Does the cell count change at matched order?** **Yes: 518 at order 1, 34 at order 6** — with containment at 100% for every order once the premise is checked. §23.10

 **What is *V* for the convex bracket on measured levels?** **It grows with order — 157 to 3.2 × 10⁶ on a matched node set.** What holds at every order is *containment*: 560 tests, zero failures. **The floor of Proposition 15.2 belongs to the classical bracket alone.** §23.10

 **Do all accelerators pay 1/\|*p*−1\|?** **Yes, at order *m*:** ***A^m*(*x^p*) = (−1)^*m* *x^p*/(*p*−1)^*m***, proved for *m* = 1…4. Richardson is the exception — a different kernel, no (*p*−1) at all. §26.6

 **Does the claim set close, and under what measure?** **Yes — a Gröbner basis of eight**, against which every relation derived since reduces to zero. E(claim set) = 0. §29.8

 **Why is *F*(−1) = 2?** **Because ℓ and *f* each have two values**, so F_box(−1) = 0, and the tree's couplings remove the vanishing factors. §11.8.1

 **Does the amplification have a general form?** **A = N[φ(Λ ∪ {*y*})] − N[φ(Λ)] − 1, exactly**, and to 7% as fibre × (0.972 − 0.154 · descendants). §16.7

 E.3 Each open item, in full
 Subordinate to E.1.2, which is the set; this is its detail, and **the two must list the same items.**
 At register 384 they did not: six open items had no entry here and one entry was an item answered at
 §12.11.1. Both repaired, and audit 15 extended so the pair cannot drift again.
 A — Sc VI 3s²3p³(⁴S°)6s ³S°₁. Committed prediction, unmeasured. [735,860, 737,380] cm⁻¹ ·
 13.5615–13.5895 nm · 91.338 eV. The lower edge tests monotonicity, the upper tests convexity.
 Needs a spectrometer.

 B — Paschen & Götze §III, Wie findet man eine Serie und ihre Grenze?, and Runge, Encyklopädie
 1926, pp. 783–820. Both unread. Springer Book Archives, public domain since 2017, scanned with OCR
 and not exposed.

 C — nine unentered literatures. Named in §29.7, with the vocabulary: excess width, Newton
 decrement, partial identification, zero-error capacity.

 D — verifying 540 Kreuzer–Skarke predictions. **Everything the slice specifies is recomputed and
 reproduces**, at register 387: 208 cells from 13 ≤ h ≤ 128 less twelve exclusions; **E(X) = 540**;
 498 join and 498 meet failures of 21,528 pairs; five χ values, only 0 and ±2 and ±4; 112 on the
 diagonal from (13,13) to (131,131); h¹¹ + h²¹ from 26 to 262. All three falsification tests hold at
 540 of 540 — every prediction has h ≥ 1 on both indices, every sum is under the published maximum of
 502, and every mirror partner is itself predicted or present. §12.11.6 supplies the calibration: an
 E(X) proposal can be vetoed by structure the index does not carry, and these are proposals of that
 kind. **What remains is one file read.** The 30,108 pairs are in a gzipped catalogue this session
 could not reach, and E.4's grid places the item exactly there — its first unmet requirement is
 *reachable from here*, not the mathematics.
 F — the reorderability decision problem, and it is not this book's problem. §30.3 establishes the
 law and the procedure. Λ's own case is answered: its structure is constructed in Chapter 7, its
 embedding is tight — height 17 = Σ(\|A_i\| − 1) — its join-irreducible poset has |J(Λ)| = 17 and width 7
 ≤ 8, and by Dilworth and Larson the chain decomposition of J(Λ) is the axis system. Nothing about Λ
 is open.

    What is open is the general question: given an arbitrary cell set with no construction,
    decide reorderability in time polynomial in \|X\| and d. §30.3.5 states the law, the procedure,
    and the residue — realisability.
 

 G — the complexity literature, entered. Named in §29.7 with its vocabulary; the question is whether
 deciding reorderability is in P, and the search has not been run in the right terms.

 H — whether Sc VI has already been measured. A literature search, not a spectrometer: the level may
 stand in a source this book has not entered, in which case item A closes by retrieval rather than by
 measurement.

 I — the spectroscopic v-check per channel. The tripwire rejects 150 of 216 offered assignments; what
 is untested is whether the seniority coordinate discriminates within a single channel, which needs
 term designations the line lists carry and this session did not fetch.


 P — the 1,442 split into independent and Ritz-derived tests. §24.9 concedes the cells rest on three
 kinds of value and only two are independently checkable. The split is arithmetic on data already
 cited, and it would tell a referee how much of the verification is circular.
E.4 The grid, read on the current set
 §2.16's grid asks, for each item, what its **first** unmet requirement is — everything below it is
 downstream. It was read once at eight items and the reading was never printed, only described.
 **It is printed here, read on the current eleven.**

  item   question                                        first unmet requirement
  A      the Sc VI level, measured                       **does the datum exist** — it does not
  B      Paschen & Götze §III, read                      is it reachable from here
  C      the nine named literatures, entered             **is the work bounded** — it is not
  D      the Kreuzer–Skarke Hodge pairs, read            is it reachable from here

    **Eight of eleven stop at the same requirement**, and it is the third: *is it reachable from
    here*. One stops earlier — the datum does not exist. Two stop later — the work is unbounded.

 **That is the grid doing what §2.16.1 claims for it.** Eight items do not present eight obstacles;
 they present one, and it is neither the hardest nor the most interesting. What blocks this book's
 open set is overwhelmingly **access** — a scan not exposed, a file not readable, a literature not
 entered — rather than difficulty or cost. **Two of the eleven are genuinely unbounded and one is
 genuinely absent; the other eight are fetching.** Registers 385, 386.

 E.4.1 Q, enumerated and closed at eight
 *Historical.* §32.2 asserted |Q| = 7. An assertion is not an enumeration, so the seven were indexed —
 blocks, obstacle, cost, depends, fibred by domain — and closed at eight. The same computation on the
 current thirteen is at E.1.2: **E(Q) = 0 in all four domain fibres and 1 unfibred.**

 E.4.2 And the column that matters
 **Every item in E.1.2 has depends = no.** Not one open question blocks a result this book relies on;
 Q is a list of what would be added, not of what is missing. That held when Q stood at eight and holds
 at thirteen — the only column whose value has never changed.

 E.6 And the complexity question itself may be settled
 Ryter & Schmid, Deciding Frattini is NP-complete, Order 11 (1994). Adams, Dwinger & Schmid,
 Maximal sublattices of finite distributive lattices, Algebra Universalis 36 (1996) 488–504.

 So decision problems about sublattices of finite distributive lattices have a complexity
 literature, and at least one of them is settled NP-complete.

    This book has not read it. §30.3's residue may already be closed there, and until that is checked
    the claim "open" is a claim about this author's reading, not about the state of knowledge.

 That is recorded as item G rather than left implicit.

 E.7 What precedence costs the book
 Nothing in §30.3 is withdrawn. The derivations agree with the literature wherever both exist, and the
 agreement is itself a check — Siggers' bijection was verified against an independent enumeration, and
 Rival's 3/2 bound contains the measured 3/4.

 What is withdrawn is the implicit claim of novelty. The section states results; it does not claim to
 state them first, and §E.5 is where the difference is written down.

    §2.10 says: enumerate targets before searching. It should have said, and now does:
    SEARCH BEFORE DERIVING. Nine components and a day, against nine citations.

 E.8 What closed, and what it cost
 Two items closed during this work. The target-spin axis was taken on the grid's advice — its first
 empty cell was reachable by labour alone — and Λ₉ passed six independent tests. §30.3's
 characterisation closed at d = 2 and its law at every d.

 The cost is in Chapter 28. Sixty-odd corrections come from §30.3 alone.

    An index that records its own rules must also record when it failed to consult them. This
    chapter is where that is done.

**E(G), recomputed by the press at every build, across the six fibres of F.2 and on the coordinates as now constrained** — 38 as first
 stated, 24 once register 367's constraint is imposed, and 23 after §12.11.0.1's four-cap table
 occupied one. Each remaining cell is a combination of definition, inputs and verification admitted by
 the structure of what the book prints and occupied by nothing it prints.

 **And seventeen of the twenty-three are cells the book should not want.** Each admitted cell was
 tested against the cells its own fibre already occupies:

  standing                                                                 cells
  **dominated** — some occupied cell of that fibre is at least as good on all three axes    **17**
  **not dominated** — genuinely above what the fibre already carries                          **6**

 A dominated cell is a *weaker* description of a number than the fibre already achieves: a bound
 recomputed but with its definition merely asserted, where the fibre already holds bounds stated with
 their inputs. **Occupying it would mean writing a worse paragraph.**

    **E(X) counts what a structure admits without asking whether occupying it is an improvement.**
    On an ordered index the admitted-and-absent set contains cells below the object's own practice
    as readily as above it, and only the second kind is work.

 **That is a third resolution §12's loop does not offer.** The loop gives two — a cell is filled by a
 number that belongs there, or excluded by a constraint the coordinates are missing. Seventeen of these
 are neither: admitted, unoccupied, and correctly so. **The actionable count for this index is six**,
 and five of the six are *verification = under variation*, which is one computation each at several cap
 settings rather than a search or a judgement. Register 368.

# Appendix F — The numbers, indexed
 Serving PART V — THE RECORD. The book's own printed numbers as a cell set — built, indexed and
 **open, and by a count the press recomputes at every build** across six fibres, and §32.1.1 flag 2 governs how a
 fibred figure should be read.

 Five things in this book were indexed before this appendix existed: its mathematics (E), its
 ignorance (F), its errors (Chapter 28), its data provenance (C) and its terms (the Index). Its numbers
 were not among them, and of the five only four were closed — E was asserted, as §D.5.1 now records.
 What is not indexed cannot be closed, and what is not closed carries defects that no audit is shaped
 to find. Register 210 was one such defect and 219 is a second, both in Appendix D and both found by trying
 to recompute rather than by reading. Two instances are too few to estimate a rate, which is why
 §F.3.1 measures the population instead.

 F.1 The population
 995 distinct claim-bearing numbers, 2,433 occurrences: 1,166 inside tables and 1,267 in prose.
 1,715 of the occurrences — 70% — stand in a paragraph carrying no method, code or section cue.
 That is not a count of unrecheckable numbers. Most are recomputable from their context. It is a
 count of numbers the book cannot currently sort into recheckable and not, which is the defect.

 F.2 The coordinates
  coordinate      values                                                            kind
  quantity        count · density · defect · bound · measurement · rate             fibre
  definition      asserted < stated < stated with inputs                            ordered
  inputs          named < enumerated < printed                                      ordered
  verification    asserted < recomputed < recomputed under variation                ordered

 Ordered coordinates fibred by the categorical one, per §D.1's precondition — the only shape ℛ
 accepts.

 F.3 The index, built
 Every claim-bearing occurrence is assigned its four coordinates mechanically from the paragraph that
 carries it — the quantity's kind from its units and vocabulary, the definition from whether a method
 and an input set are both named, the inputs from whether they are named, enumerated or printed, and
 the verification from whether the paragraph records a recomputation and whether that recomputation
 varied the caps. The rules are stated so the assignment can be rerun; they are coarse, and being
 coarse and rerunnable is the point, since a judgment made once per number would not be checkable at
 all.

  fibre          occurrences   distinct cells   admitted   E
  count               1,680               14         22    8
  defect                291               15         24    9
  measurement           204               12         20    8
  bound                 183                9         13    4
  rate                   56                9         13    4
  density                39                5          5    0
  all                 2,453               —          —    33

 **E(G) is recomputed by the press at every build**, across the six fibres of F.2 and on the coordinates as now constrained — because the press at every build, since a figure about the book goes stale the moment the book is written into. Thirty-three combinations of definition, inputs and
 verification are admitted by the
 structure of what the book already prints and occupied by nothing it prints. Each is a number the book
 could state to the standard it already meets elsewhere and does not — and by §12's loop each resolves
 either by a number that belongs there or by a constraint the coordinates are missing.

 **The second route was tried three times and succeeded once.**

 **Refuted twice.** That a recomputed number must carry a stated definition, and that it must carry at
 least enumerated inputs. Both are principled and **both are contradicted by cells the book already
 occupies** — four numbers recorded as recomputed while their definition is only asserted, three while
 their inputs are only named. A constraint refuted by its own extent is dead, and those seven cells are
 what the failure leaves behind: **a recomputation whose definition is not stated is not checkable.**

 **Established once.** The definition and inputs axes are **not independent**, and this is provable
 from F.3's own assignment rules rather than judged. *Definition = stated* is assigned exactly when a
 method is named and an input set is not; *inputs = enumerated* is assigned exactly when an input set
 is named. The two cannot hold together, so **definition = stated forbids inputs = enumerated** — a
 constraint refuting nothing the book prints, and removing fourteen cells.

 **The first two attempts failed because they asked what a number ought to satisfy. The third asked
 what the assignment rules already forbid.**

 **And the figure moves as the book is written.** It has read 33, 38, 24 and 21 within one session,
 every move caused by writing into the book it measures. **A number about the book cannot be printed as
 prose without going stale**, so the press recomputes it at every build and prints it beside the
 tripwire. Registers 366, 367, 368. Unlike Q, this index is open and is not claimed otherwise.

 F.3.1 And the distribution is the finding
 Of 2,453 indexed occurrences, **2,316 carry verification = asserted** — no recomputation recorded
 anywhere near them — against 115 recomputed and 22 recomputed under variation. On the definition
 axis, 1,998 are asserted, 145 stated, and 310 stated with their inputs. So 94% of the numbers this
 book prints are, by its own coordinates, at the weakest verification the coordinate admits. That is
 not a claim that 94% are wrong. It is a measurement of how much of the book's arithmetic stands on
 the reader's trust rather than on the book's own standard, and it is the first time the quantity has
 had a number.

 F.3.2 The tripwire
 The press runs the extraction on every render and reports the count before any reading — a D1-type
 check in Chapter 16's sense: mechanical, prior, and indifferent to what the author believes. It
 reports and does not fail the build, because a failing build on 94% would stop the book from printing
 at all. The threshold is a decision the author has not made, and recording that it is undecided is
 better than choosing it silently.

 F.3.3 The closure, represented as it must be
 A count of what this book omits is computed **from** this book, so writing the count into the book
 changes what it counts. E(G) read 33, 38, 24, 23 and 21 within one session and every move was caused
 by the writing. **The count is the wrong representation, and it is wrong for a reason the book already
 states.**

 §12.11.3.1 divides bounds by where they come from. **A bound from a law is stable; a bound from an
 extent moves with the extent.** E(G) is an extent quantity — of course it moves. What is law-derived
 here does not move, and measured across three states of the book, one before the third companion
 paper's intake, one before the register was compressed, and one now:

  what is asserted                                                          before intake   before compression   now
  E(G), the count                                                                      23                   21    23
  **the constraint** — definition = stated forbids inputs = enumerated               holds                holds  holds
  **the three refutations** — recomputed ⇏ stated, ⇏ enumerated, ⇏ both              hold                 hold   hold
  **combinations admitted and absent** — nine of an eleven-element union             all nine             all nine  all nine

 **So the closure is represented by three things, and the count is not one of them.**

    **First, the constraint**, which is a fact about F.3's assignment rules and cannot be made false
    by writing: *definition = stated forbids inputs = enumerated*. **Second, the three refutations**,
    which are facts about what the book already prints and would need a withdrawal to overturn.
    **Third, the nine combinations** that stand admitted and absent in every state of the book —
    these are the shape of the omission, and they are what a reader should be given.

 The nine are: a number **recomputed while its definition is only asserted**, at each of the three input
 standards; a number recomputed **under variation** with its definition asserted, at two of them; and a
 number whose definition is *stated* or *stated with inputs* while its inputs are **printed**, at each
 verification level. Two further combinations come and go with the text and are named as unstable
 rather than counted.

 **The count remains, printed by the press at each build rather than by the author** — a readout, not
 a claim. **This is the law–extent rule turned on the book's own index**: state what the rules forbid
 and what the extent has refuted, and let the extent's own arithmetic be computed rather than written.
 Register 370.

 F.4 What closing it would establish, and what it costs
 D.6 claims nothing is missing that the structure admits, about the book's mathematics. Closing G
 extends that from the mathematics to the arithmetic, and makes §32.2's S3 — that a reader given the
 claims and the evidence could reconstruct the structure — true in the strong sense rather than the
 prose one. The cost is E.4-shaped: buildable, days to unbounded, depends = no. Entered in Q as
 item M, enumerated rather than asserted away.

 F.4.1 Two ratios the book had never measured about itself
 Appendix F indexes the numbers this book prints and had not indexed the two that describe its own
 shape. They are named here because a quantity without a name cannot be tracked, and both are
 measurements the method demands of anything else it examines.

    **The withdrawal ratio.** Words of register against words of argument — Chapter 28 against every other chapter of the book. **24,162 : 54,241 = 0.445.** One word withdrawn for every two
    asserted.

    **The method ratio.** Words of Part I — the twenty principles, the twenty-four protocols,
    the twenty-five prime audits — against the whole book but for itself. **11,811 : 75,459 = 0.157.** Against the
    other five appendices alone it is 0.877: the machinery is now very nearly the size of all the
    mathematics it audits.

 **What each is for.** §32.1.1's fourth referee flag says the register is longer than the results and
 the book has no answer to the fair version of the question. The withdrawal ratio is that question
 made numeric: at 0.502 the register is half the argument, and the flag asked not whether that is
 large but **whether it is falling**. It is not tracked over time and cannot be, because entries carry
 no date; §32.1.2's split of thirty-four defects to eight self-inflicted is one session's measurement
 and there is no second point. **A ratio with one observation is a number, not a trend**, and that is
 the honest state of referee flag 4.

 The method ratio has no referee behind it and may matter more. Part I has grown faster in these
 sessions than anything it governs. Whether a method that is 88% the weight of its own subject is
 thorough or top-heavy is a judgement in §2.18's sense and not a computation — but it is now a
 judgement with a figure attached, which it was not. Register 296.

 F.4.2 The ideal withdrawal ratio is 1 : 1
 §F.4.1 measures the withdrawal ratio in words and leaves the number uninterpreted. Words are a proxy.
 The quantity is a ratio of **claims**, and on that basis the ideal has a derivation rather than a
 preference.

    **A claim's survival carries information, and 1 : 1 is where it carries the most.** If
    withdrawals and survivors stand in ratio r, the chance a given claim survives is 1/(1 + r), and
    the news that it did carries −p log₂p − (1−p)log₂(1−p) bits. That is maximal at p = ½, which is
    r = 1. **Below 1 : 1 survival is cheap news** — almost everything survives, so surviving says
    little. **Above it, withdrawal is cheap** — the record is mostly failure and the process is
    spending more than it keeps. At parity, the fact that a claim stands is worth exactly one bit,
    and that is the most a register can ever tell a reader about any single claim in it.

 **Measured, the book does not have one answer. It has a bracket.** 190 claim-numbers stand in the
 register. What stands against them depends entirely on what a surviving claim is, and the book has
 never said:

  surviving claims taken as                     count      ratio        p(survive)   bits
  Appendix D's mathematical elements               36     5.28 : 1          0.159    0.633
  those plus the Index's terms                     87     2.18 : 1          0.314    0.898
  every claim-bearing number in Appendix F      2,831     0.07 : 1          0.937    0.339

 **The three readings span a factor of seventy-five, and 1 : 1 lies inside them.** That is a bracket
 in this book's own sense: the quantity is bounded by measured neighbours and undetermined between
 them, so the prediction is **consistent with the evidence and not established by it** — which is
 precisely what §15 says a bracket means and precisely the standing §25.6 has.

 **What would settle it is a definition, not a measurement.** §D.5.3 closed the oldest item in Q by
 saying what an element is; the same move is available here and has not been made. Until *claim* is
 defined as *element* was, the withdrawal ratio has no value, only a range — and the register, which
 exists to make the book's failures countable, cannot count its own denominator. Entered in Q.
 Register 297.

 F.4.3 Time, which this book already has and does not read
 §F.4.2 leaves the withdrawal ratio with one observation, and §32.1.1's fourth flag asks whether it is
 falling. Both need time. The book has it.

 **The clock is the entry number.** It is monotone in time, and the register became a series the moment
 register 290 sorted it ascending. The drift from 29 words an entry to 149 — register 309 — is a
 reading of that series, taken by hand, with nothing added.

 **What is missing is a denominator, not a clock.** An ordinal gives *later*; a rate needs *per what*.
 Flag 4 asks for withdrawals per claim made, and claims-made-per-session is the one quantity no index
 here records. That single addition makes the flag answerable and nothing else does.

 **Time is trivially admissible, which is why nothing ever forced it.** By §18.4.1's criterion a
 coordinate is admissible when its cap is a morphism for the operation preserved. Time caps nothing
 and is capped by nothing: a free chain, a bare product factor, closure-preserving in any index here
 by construction. Cheap to add, and therefore never demanded by the structure.

 **It must not enter Λ.** Λ's cells are transitions and a transition has no date; a time coordinate
 would silently convert Λ into an index of *observations* of transitions, which is a different object
 with different physics. Nothing in §7.1 currently prevents this and §7.1 now says so.

 **Where it belongs, and what each place buys.** The register, as a session column — the rate, and
 flag 4. Appendices E, F and G, as *entered at* and *changed at* — which is exactly what register 307's
 stratigraphy was demanding: E.1.1's lineage and §3.8's growth table are time introduced ad hoc,
 twice, because the coordinate was absent. With the column they become projections instead of prose.

 **And one thing not to mistake for a clock.** Status — withdrawn < conjectured < measured < verified
 < proved — reads like time and is not. Register 219 ran proved → withdrawn → proved across three
 entries. **Status is not monotone in time**, so it cannot serve as the clock, and this book has been
 half-using it as one since §D.2 defined it. Register 311.

 F.5 And it needs its own falsification
 An index that cannot fail is not a check. The test: sample n printed numbers, attempt recomputation
 from the book alone, report the rate. This session is the first such sample, n = 8 — seven quantities
 recomputed from the construction and Appendix D's E = 0 — and the observed failure is one in eight,
 Appendix D, whose inputs the book does not print. A rate from eight is not a rate; it is a method
 demonstrated once, and F.3.1 gives the population it should be drawn from.

# END MATTER

# Index
 This index satisfies the law of this book.

 Construction
 Terms are ordered by specificity: s ⊑ t when s is a specialisation of t. The index is closed iff

    s ⊑ t ⇒ loc(s) ⊆ loc(t)

 every location of a specialisation being also a location of its generalisation. That is a down-set condition in §16.4 form, so **a closed index is a sublattice of (terms × locations)**.

 **Verified:** 51 terms, 129 entries, 40 specialisation relations, **0 violations — E(index) = 0.**

 **What closure buys.** The term list is recoverable by projection (S1); the specificity order is recoverable by tree propagation, a term hierarchy being a tree (S2); loc(·) is recoverable as the union over specialisations (S3); and every (term, location) pair is present or absent — **no *see also* limbo, no partial entries** (D3).

 **An index with E > 0 requires the reader to already know where to look — which is the periodic table's failure at the scale of a back-matter page.**

 Bergman, G. M. — the Double-projection Theorem: a sublattice of a finite product of lattices is

 R.7 What is not cited, and why
 No source is cited for the lattice itself. Λ is constructed in Chapter 7 from the four quantum numbers
 and the exclusion principle, both standard, and every structural result about it in this book was
 computed rather than looked up. §24 states what was searched for and not found.

 **Physical constants** — *R* = 109,737.31568 cm⁻¹ — are used without citation, as a metre is.
